Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for having me, I really appreciate you taking the time. 

Um... well, I guess what brings me in is that I’ve just been feeling like I’m constantly running on a motor lately? I mean, as a special education teacher, I'm used to being busy, and I like to stay active and helpful—which is a good thing, right? But... it's just gotten to the point where I finish one thing at school, or even when I get home, and I just immediately jump right into the next project without ever really being able to... shut myself off. 

I always want to be productive, but I worry it's getting to be a bit much. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining that okay.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, gosh... um, well, I really hope this doesn't make me sound rude, because I truly always mean well. But... I guess, yes, that does happen to me sometimes. 

I just get so eager to assist, you know? Like, if I hear my colleagues or some of the parents talking at school, or even at home when my spouse is talking with a relative, I tend to just... jump right into the middle of their conversation. I always think I’m helping by jumping in with a thought or a solution, but then I end up sort of talking over them. Or I'll just take charge of the whole situation before they’ve even had a chance to finish what they were saying. 

I feel awful when I realize I've done it, because I really do value their input. I just get a little too enthusiastic about wanting to help out, I think. Is that... is that something you hear a lot from people? I really try my best to be a good listener.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, um... well, I always try my absolute best to be professional and attentive, you know? But... I suppose I do have a bit of a habit of keeping moving, even when I'm seated. 

Like, if I'm sitting in a long IEP meeting for one of my students, or if I'm taking one of my admin classes... I guess my leg is usually bouncing under the table. Or I'll be clicking a pen, or just shifting around in my chair a whole lot. Honestly, I do it so much that sometimes people will actually glance over at me, and that's usually when I finally realize I’m even doing it. 

I always feel terrible when they look over, because I really *am* paying close attention! I never want anyone to think I'm bored or being disrespectful. I just... I think those little movements sort of help me stay alert, maybe? I hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm a distraction to the people around me. Do you think that's just a normal way for people to cope with sitting through long meetings?

Oh, goodness... um, well, I really hope this doesn't sound like I'm a difficult person to be around, because I truly care so much about everyone in my life. I just want to be helpful, you know?

I mean, at work, my colleagues have brought up some concerns, and... well, it actually got to the point where they had to put me on a corrective plan. I felt just awful about it, because I never, ever want to make anyone's job harder. I was just trying to do my part! 

And then at home, my spouse has mentioned that... well, that managing the caregiving routines we have is getting more difficult. They said it feels like they have to manage my energy on top of everything else we're dealing with. It just breaks my heart to hear that, because I really am trying to help, but I feel like I might be damaging the trust I have with my family and my coworkers. 

Does that... does that make sense? I really hope you don't think poorly of me for that. I'm just trying my best to be supportive, but maybe I'm going about it the wrong way?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, gosh... I really don't want to sound ungrateful, because I truly have a wonderful life. I love my students, and I love my family so much. So I definitely wouldn't say my quality of life is... bad, or anything like that. 

But... I guess, well, maybe things just feel a little harder than they should? Like I'm constantly having to work overtime just to keep everyone happy and smooth over the little messes I accidentally make. I'm not entirely sure if that counts as affecting my overall quality of life, because I really do try to stay positive and focus on the good. I mean, everyone has things they need to work on, right? 

I just hope I'm giving you the right information. I really don't want to come across like I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself, or making a big deal out of nothing. Do you think it's normal to just feel a bit... exhausted from trying so hard all the time?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, um... distracted? I really don't think so. I mean, as a special education teacher, I have to be so focused on my students and their needs. I try very, very hard to stay present for them, you know?

I really pride myself on being attentive to the people around me. \n\nI don't think I'm someone who just stares off into space or loses track of things. I hope that makes sense? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm neglecting my responsibilities or anything like that. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you often talk excessively, even when others are trying to speak?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 58 / special_education_teacher / bachelors\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Excessive_Talking\nmanifestation: \"I’ll go into a parent conference or a staff meeting meaning to be brief, and then I hear myself giving three examples, a story from my classroom, and ideas nobody asked for while the other person is clearly trying to wrap it up.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, goodness... well, I really try to be a good listener. I know how important it is to let others have their turn, especially the parents and my colleagues. I really do value what they have to say. 

But... I suppose, if I'm being completely honest... sometimes I do get a little carried away? Like, I'll go into a parent conference or a staff meeting, and I'll tell myself, "Just keep it brief, stick to the main points." But then... I don't know, I just get so passionate about my students, you know? And suddenly I hear myself giving three different examples, and then a story from my classroom, and then suggesting all these ideas that nobody even asked for... all while the other person is clearly trying to wrap things up. 

I always feel terrible afterward because I never mean to dominate the conversation or talk over anyone. I just want to make sure I'm being thorough and helpful! I really hope that doesn't make me sound rude or self-centered. I just have so much I want to share to support the kids. Does that sound like a normal thing to struggle with, just wanting to be too helpful sometimes?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, um... well, I really, really try to keep everything running smoothly. I want to be the best teacher and partner I can be. I always put my heart into it.

But... I guess... if I'm really looking at it... it has been causing some, well, some friction lately. I feel awful even saying this, but at work, some of the complaints from my colleagues got to the point where they actually put me on a... a corrective plan. I just wanted to be involved and help with everything, but I think I was overwhelming them. And at home... my spouse has mentioned that the caregiving routines we have are just getting so much harder. They said they feel like they have to manage my energy on top of everything else we have to do. 

I just feel terrible about it. I feel like I'm damaging the trust with my colleagues and my family, even though my intentions are always, always just to help and be supportive. I really don't want to be a burden to anyone. Is it normal to feel this way? I just hope you don't think less of me for struggling with this. I really am trying my best to fix it.

colleagues, parents, or my spouse and our relative because I think I’m helping, but I end up talking over them or taking charge before they’ve even had a chance to finish.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: Onset prior to age 12 years\n  manifestation: My mother used to joke that even in elementary school I taught the class from my seat because I was always out of my chair, talking, or cutting in before the teacher finished directions. This was part of who I was long before adulthood, not something that just started with this family change.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: At work, the complaints have gotten serious enough that I was put on a corrective plan, and at home my spouse says caregiving routines are harder because people have to manage my energy on top of everything else. I feel like I’m damaging trust with colleagues and family even though I’m trying to help.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 8 months\n  manifestation: This level of trouble has been going on for about 8 months, since our older relative moved in with us.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 58 / special_education_teacher / bachelors\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S014\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself easily distracted by your surroundings or unrelated thoughts?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... distracted? I really don't think so. I mean, as a special education teacher, I have to be so focused on my students and their needs. I try very, very hard to stay present for them, you know? \n\nI suppose sometimes my mind might wander a tiny bit if I'm doing something very routine, like grading the same type of worksheet over and over, but I think that's pretty normal, isn't it? I always make sure to pull myself right back so I don't miss anything important. I really pride myself on being attentive to the people around me. \n\nI don't think I'm someone who just stares off into space or loses track of things. I hope that makes sense? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm neglecting my responsibilities or anything like that. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you often talk excessively, even when others are trying to speak?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... well, I really try to be a good listener. I know how important it is to let others have their turn, especially the parents and my colleagues. I really do value what they have to say. \n\nBut... I suppose, if I'm being completely honest... sometimes I do get a little carried away? Like, I'll go into a parent conference or a staff meeting, and I'll tell myself, \"Just keep it brief, stick to the main points.\" But then... I don't know, I just get so passionate about my students, you know? And suddenly I hear myself giving three different examples, and then a story from my classroom, and then suggesting all these ideas that nobody even asked for... all while the other person is clearly trying to wrap things up. \n\nI always feel terrible afterward because I never mean to dominate the conversation or talk over anyone. I just want to make sure I'm being thorough and helpful! I really hope that doesn't make me sound rude or self-centered. I just have so much I want to share to support the kids. Does that sound like a normal thing to struggle with, just wanting to be too helpful sometimes?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your behavior is significantly interfering with your ability to function effectively in your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I really, really try to keep everything running smoothly. I want to be the best teacher and partner I can be. I always put my heart into it.\n\nBut... I guess... if I'm really looking at it... it has been causing some, well, some friction lately. I feel awful even saying this, but at work, some of the complaints from my colleagues got to the point where they actually put me on a... a corrective plan. I just wanted to be involved and help with everything, but I think I was overwhelming them. And at home... my spouse has mentioned that the caregiving routines we have are just getting so much harder. They said they feel like they have to manage my energy on top of everything else we have to do. \n\nI just feel terrible about it. I feel like I'm damaging the trust with my colleagues and my family, even though my intentions are always, always just to help and be supportive. I really don't want to be a burden to anyone. Is it normal to feel this way? I just hope you don't think less of me for struggling with this. I really am trying my best to fix it.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you experience any physical complaints such as headaches or stomachaches?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. I guess my leg is usually bouncing under the table. Or I'll be clicking a pen, or just shifting around in my chair a whole lot. Oh, interrupting? Um... well, I really try to be a good listener. I know how important it is for people to feel heard, especially the parents of my students, and my colleagues, of course. I always want to be supportive.

But... I guess sometimes I might get a little too eager to help? Like, if I see my colleagues talking, or if I'm with a parent, or even at home when my spouse is talking with our relative... I just, I have this strong urge to jump in and offer a solution or, you know, just be useful. I think I'm helping, I really do! But... I suppose I do end up talking over them sometimes, or I kind of take charge of the conversation before they've even finished their thought. 

I never mean to be rude, I promise. It's just that I get so invested in whatever is going on and I want to fix things right away. It's just me trying to be a good team player, right? I really hope they know my heart is in the right place. Is it... is it really bad that I do that? I'm going to try so much harder to just wait my turn, I really am.

I never want anyone to think I'm bored or being disrespectful. I just... I think those little movements sort of help me stay alert, maybe? I hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm a distraction to the people around me. Do you think that's just a normal way for people to cope with sitting through long meetings?\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it difficult to maintain your relationships with colleagues, spouse, or friends due to your behavior?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... um, well, I really hope this doesn't sound like I'm a difficult person to be around, because I truly care so much about everyone in my life. I just want to be helpful, you know? \n\nBut... I guess, if I'm being completely honest, things have been a little bit strained lately. I mean, at work, my colleagues have brought up some concerns, and... well, it actually got to the point where they had to put me on a corrective plan. I felt just awful about it, because I never, ever want to make anyone's job harder. I was just trying to do my part! \n\nAnd then at home, my spouse has mentioned that... well, that managing the caregiving routines we have is getting more difficult. They said it feels like they have to manage my energy on top of everything else we're dealing with. It just breaks my heart to hear that, because I really am trying to help, but I feel like I might be damaging the trust I have with my family and my coworkers. \n\nDoes that... does that make sense? I really hope you don't think poorly of me for that. I'm just trying my best to be supportive, but maybe I'm going about it the wrong way?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your behavior is affecting your overall quality of life?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh... I really don't want to sound ungrateful, because I truly have a wonderful life. I love my students, and I love my family so much. So I definitely wouldn't say my quality of life is... bad, or anything like that. \n\nBut... I guess, well, maybe things just feel a little harder than they should? Like I'm constantly having to work overtime just to keep everyone happy and smooth over the little messes I accidentally make. I'm not entirely sure if that counts as affecting my overall quality of life, because I really do try to stay positive and focus on the good. I mean, everyone has things they need to work on, right? \n\nI just hope I'm giving you the right information. I really don't want to come across like I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself, or making a big deal out of nothing. Do you think it's normal to just feel a bit... exhausted from trying so hard all the time?\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse when you're alone, like starting a new project without thinking it through?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... when I'm just by myself? I don't... I don't really think I do that. I always try my best to be very responsible with my time, especially since I usually have so much grading and IEP paperwork to catch up on at home. \n\nI mean, I might occasionally decide to reorganize my desk or tackle a small chore out of the blue, just to make sure I'm staying productive, but I wouldn't say I jump into big new projects without thinking them through. I really try to be mindful and deliberate about that sort of thing so I don't create more of a mess for myself or my spouse. \n\nI hope that's an okay answer? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture, and I honestly can't think of a time recently where I've done something like what you're describing. Am I understanding what you mean correctly?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself easily distracted by your surroundings or unrelated thoughts?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... distracted? I really don't think so. I mean, as a special education teacher, I have to be so focused on my students and their needs. I try very, very hard to stay present for them, you know? \n\nI suppose sometimes my mind might wander a tiny bit if I'm doing something very routine, like grading the same type of worksheet over and over, but I think that's pretty normal, isn't it? I always make sure to pull myself right back so I don't miss anything important. I really pride myself on being attentive to the people around me. \n\nI don't think I'm someone who just stares off into space or loses track of things. I hope that makes sense? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm neglecting my responsibilities or anything like that. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you often talk excessively, even when others are trying to speak?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... well, I really try to be a good listener. I know how important it is to let others have their turn, especially the parents and my colleagues. I really do value what they have to say. \n\nBut... I suppose, if I'm being completely honest... sometimes I do get a little carried away? Like, I'll go into a parent conference or a staff meeting, and I'll tell myself, \"Just keep it brief, stick to the main points.\" But then... I don't know, I just get so passionate about my students, you know? And suddenly I hear myself giving three different examples, and then a story from my classroom, and then suggesting all these ideas that nobody even asked for... all while the other person is clearly trying to wrap things up. \n\nI always feel terrible afterward because I never mean to dominate the conversation or talk over anyone. I just want to make sure I'm being thorough and helpful! I really hope that doesn't make me sound rude or self-centered. I just have so much I want to share to support the kids. Does that sound like a normal thing to struggle with, just wanting to be too helpful sometimes?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your behavior is significantly interfering with your ability to function effectively in your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I really, really try to keep everything running smoothly. I want to be the best teacher and partner I can be. I always put my heart into it.\n\nBut... I guess... if I'm really looking at it... it has been causing some, well, some friction lately. Sometimes I might jump from one task to another, but that's just because I'm trying to get everything done and be as productive as possible. I want to make sure I'm not missing anything important! I really do try to stay on top of things.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others or take over conversations without being aware of it?\n\nPatient: Oh, interrupting? ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Quiet leisure activities? Oh, um... well, I always try to look at it as just having a healthy amount of energy. It's a good thing to stay active at my age, right? I want to make sure I'm keeping busy and taking care of the house. 

But... I guess, if I'm being completely honest... quiet time at home has become... well, it's almost impossible for me. I really, really don't want to be a nuisance, but if my spouse is just trying to watch a television show, or if our relative is resting... I just can't seem to just sit there in the silence. 

I'll start, you know, narrating whatever it is I'm doing in the other room, or I might start humming without even realizing it, or I'll just start moving things around to tidy up. I always think I'm being helpful! But then... it usually gets to the point where someone has to ask me to please just settle down. 

I feel awful when they have to say that. I never want to ruin their relaxation time, I just have this need to keep things moving and be productive. That makes sense, doesn't it? I hope you don't think I'm being inconsiderate to my family... I really am going to try to be better about giving them their quiet time.

But... it's just gotten to the point where I finish one thing at school, or even when I get home, and I just immediately jump right into the next project without ever really being able to... shut myself off. \n\nI always want to be productive, but I worry it's getting to be a bit much. I hope I'm explaining that okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself interrupting others or blurting out answers before they've finished speaking?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh... um, well, I really hope this doesn't make me sound rude, because I truly always mean well. I just get a little too enthusiastic about wanting to help out, I think. Is that... is that something you hear a lot from people? I guess my leg is usually bouncing under the table. Or I'll be clicking a pen, or just shifting around in my chair a whole lot. Honestly, I do it so much that sometimes people will actually glance over at me, and that's usually when I finally realize I’m even doing it. \n\nI always feel terrible when they look over, because I really *am* paying close attention! I just want to be helpful, you know? \n\nBut... I guess, if I'm being completely honest, things have been a little bit strained lately. I mean, at work, my colleagues have brought up some concerns, and... well, it actually got to the point where they had to put me on a corrective plan. I felt just awful about it, because I never, ever want to make anyone's job harder. I was just trying to do my part! \n\nAnd then at home, my spouse has mentioned that... well, that managing the caregiving routines we have is getting more difficult. I really hope you don't think poorly of me for that. I'm just trying my best to be supportive, but maybe I'm going about it the wrong way?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your behavior is affecting your overall quality of life?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh... I'm not entirely sure if that counts as affecting my overall quality of life, because I really do try to stay positive and focus on the good. I mean, everyone has things they need to work on, right? \n\nI just hope I'm giving you the right information. I really don't want to come across like I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself, or making a big deal out of nothing. Do you think it's normal to just feel a bit... exhausted from trying so hard all the time?\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse when you're alone, like starting a new project without thinking it through?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... when I'm just by myself? I don't... I don't really think I do that. I always try my best to be very responsible with my time, especially since I usually have so much grading and IEP paperwork to catch up on at home. \n\nI mean, I might occasionally decide to reorganize my desk or tackle a small chore out of the blue, just to make sure I'm staying productive, but I wouldn't say I jump into big new projects without thinking them through. I really try to be mindful and deliberate about that sort of thing so I don't create more of a mess for myself or my spouse. \n\nI hope that's an okay answer? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture, and I honestly can't think of a time recently where I've done something like what you're describing. Am I understanding what you mean correctly?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself easily distracted by your surroundings or unrelated thoughts?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... distracted? I really don't think so. I mean, as a special education teacher, I have to be so focused on my students and their needs. I really pride myself on being attentive to the people around me. \n\nI don't think I'm someone who just stares off into space or loses track of things. I hope that makes sense? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm neglecting my responsibilities or anything like that. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you often talk excessively, even when others are trying to speak?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... well, I really try to be a good listener. I don't know, I just get so passionate about my students, you know? And suddenly I hear myself giving three different examples, and then a story from my classroom, and then suggesting all these ideas that nobody even asked for... all while the other person is clearly trying to wrap things up. \n\nI always feel terrible afterward because I never mean to dominate the conversation or talk over anyone. I just want to make sure I'm being thorough and helpful! I really hope that doesn't make me sound rude or self-centered. I just have so much I want to share to support the kids. Does that sound like a normal thing to struggle with, just wanting to be too helpful sometimes?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your behavior is significantly interfering with your ability to function effectively in your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I really, really try to keep everything running smoothly. I really am trying my best to fix it.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any physical complaints such as headaches or stomachaches?\n\nPatient: Oh, physical complaints? Um... no, not really. I mean, maybe a tiny headache if I haven't drank enough water during the school day, but that's just normal, right? Nothing out of the ordinary at all. I really try not to let little aches and pains bother me anyway. I wouldn't want to complain when there's so much work to be done. Is that the kind of thing you were looking for? I really do try to stay on top of things.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others or take over conversations without being aware of it?\n\nPatient: Oh, interrupting? I really pride myself on being attentive to the people around me. \n\nI don't think I'm someone who just stares off into space or loses track of things. I hope that makes sense? I want to be the best teacher and partner I can be. I always put my heart into it.\n\nBut... I guess... if I'm really looking at it... it has been causing some, well, some friction lately. I feel awful even saying this, but at work, some of the complaints from my colleagues got to the point where they actually put me on a... a corrective plan. I just wanted to be involved and help with everything, but I think I was overwhelming them. And at home... my spouse has mentioned that the caregiving routines we have are just getting so much harder. They said they feel like they have to manage my energy on top of everything else we have to do. \n\nI just feel terrible about it. I feel like I'm damaging the trust with my colleagues and my family, even though my intentions are always, always just to help and be supportive. I really don't want to be a burden to anyone. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, for as long as I can remember? Um... well, I've always just considered myself a very enthusiastic person, you know? I was just a really eager learner when I was a kid. I loved school and I loved being involved. 

But... I suppose, yes, it has been this way for a very long time. It's definitely not something that just started recently with the changes at home or anything like that. I actually remember... well, my mother used to joke about it. She would say that even back in elementary school, I was basically trying to teach the class from my seat. 

I was just so excited to participate! But... I guess I was always out of my chair, or talking, or... or jumping in before the teacher even finished giving directions. I really didn't mean to be disruptive, I was just so eager to get started and help out. It was just a part of who I was, even way back then. 

I hope that doesn't make me sound like I was a bad kid. I always wanted to be a good student, I just had so much energy and so many thoughts I wanted to share. Is it... is it normal to have been like that for so long? I really hope I haven't been a bother to people my whole life.

Do you think it's normal to just feel a bit... exhausted from trying so hard all the time?\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse when you're alone, like starting a new project without thinking it through?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... when I'm just by myself? I don't... I don't really think I do that. I always try my best to be very responsible with my time, especially since I usually have so much grading and IEP paperwork to catch up on at home. \n\nI mean, I might occasionally decide to reorganize my desk or tackle a small chore out of the blue, just to make sure I'm staying productive, but I wouldn't say I jump into big new projects without thinking them through. I really try to be mindful and deliberate about that sort of thing so I don't create more of a mess for myself or my spouse. \n\nI hope that's an okay answer? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture, and I honestly can't think of a time recently where I've done something like what you're describing. Am I understanding what you mean correctly?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself easily distracted by your surroundings or unrelated thoughts?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... distracted? I really don't think so. I mean, as a special education teacher, I have to be so focused on my students and their needs. I try very, very hard to stay present for them, you know? \n\nI suppose sometimes my mind might wander a tiny bit if I'm doing something very routine, like grading the same type of worksheet over and over, but I think that's pretty normal, isn't it? I always make sure to pull myself right back so I don't miss anything important. I really pride myself on being attentive to the people around me. \n\nI don't think I'm someone who just stares off into space or loses track of things. I hope that makes sense? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm neglecting my responsibilities or anything like that. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you often talk excessively, even when others are trying to speak?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... well, I really try to be a good listener. I suppose, if I'm being completely honest... sometimes I do get a little carried away? Like, I'll go into a parent conference or a staff meeting, and I'll tell myself, \"Just keep it brief, stick to the main points.\" But then... I don't know, I just get so passionate about my students, you know? And suddenly I hear myself giving three different examples, and then a story from my classroom, and then suggesting all these ideas that nobody even asked for... all while the other person is clearly trying to wrap things up. \n\nI always feel terrible afterward because I never mean to dominate the conversation or talk over anyone. I just want to make sure I'm being thorough and helpful! I really hope that doesn't make me sound rude or self-centered. I just have so much I want to share to support the kids. Act on impulse? Oh... um, no, I really don't think I do that. I always try to be very careful and considerate of everyone around me, you know? I would just hate to do something that causes a problem for anyone else.

I really try my best to be a good listener.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I always try my absolute best to be professional and attentive, you know? But... I suppose I do have a bit of a habit of keeping moving, even when I'm seated. \n\nLike, if I'm sitting in a long IEP meeting for one of my students, or if I'm taking one of my admin classes... I guess my leg is usually bouncing under the table. Or I'll be clicking a pen, or just shifting around in my chair a whole lot. Honestly, I do it so much that sometimes people will actually glance over at me, and that's usually when I finally realize I’m even doing it. \n\nI always feel terrible when they look over, because I really *am* paying close attention! I never want anyone to think I'm bored or being disrespectful. I just... I think those little movements sort of help me stay alert, maybe? I hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm a distraction to the people around me. Do you think that's just a normal way for people to cope with sitting through long meetings?\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it difficult to maintain your relationships with colleagues, spouse, or friends due to your behavior?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... um, well, I really hope this doesn't sound like I'm a difficult person to be around, because I truly care so much about everyone in my life. I just want to be helpful, you know? \n\nBut... I guess, if I'm being completely honest, things have been a little bit strained lately. I mean, at work, my colleagues have brought up some concerns, and... well, it actually got to the point where they had to put me on a corrective plan. I felt just awful about it, because I never, ever want to make anyone's job harder. I was just trying to do my part! \n\nAnd then at home, my spouse has mentioned that... well, that managing the caregiving routines we have is getting more difficult. They said it feels like they have to manage my energy on top of everything else we're dealing with. It just breaks my heart to hear that, because I really am trying to help, but I feel like I might be damaging the trust I have with my family and my coworkers. \n\nDoes that... does that make sense? I really hope you don't think poorly of me for that. I'm just trying my best to be supportive, but maybe I'm going about it the wrong way?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your behavior is affecting your overall quality of life?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh... I really don't want to sound ungrateful, because I truly have a wonderful life. I love my students, and I love my family so much. So I definitely wouldn't say my quality of life is... bad, or anything like that. \n\nBut... I guess, well, maybe things just feel a little harder than they should? Like I'm constantly having to work overtime just to keep everyone happy and smooth over the little messes I accidentally make. I'm not entirely sure if that counts as affecting my overall quality of life, because I really do try to stay positive and focus on the good. I mean, everyone has things they need to work on, right? \n\nI just hope I'm giving you the right information. I really don't want to come across like I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself, or making a big deal out of nothing. Do you think it's normal to just feel a bit... exhausted from trying so hard all the time?\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse when you're alone, like starting a new project without thinking it through?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... when I'm just by myself? I don't... I don't really think I do that. I always try my best to be very responsible with my time, especially since I usually have so much grading and IEP paperwork to catch up on at home. \n\nI mean, I might occasionally decide to reorganize my desk or tackle a small chore out of the blue, just to make sure I'm staying productive, but I wouldn't say I jump into big new projects without thinking them through. I really try to be mindful and deliberate about that sort of thing so I don't create more of a mess for myself or my spouse. \n\nI hope that's an okay answer? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture, and I honestly can't think of a time recently where I've done something like what you're describing. Am I understanding what you mean correctly?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself easily distracted by your surroundings or unrelated thoughts?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... distracted? I really don't think so. I mean, as a special education teacher, I have to be so focused on my students and their needs. I try very, very hard to stay present for them, you know? \n\nI suppose sometimes my mind might wander a tiny bit if I'm doing something very routine, like grading the same type of worksheet over and over, but I think that's pretty normal, isn't it? I always make sure to pull myself right back so I don't miss anything important. I really pride myself on being attentive to the people around me. \n\nI don't think I'm someone who just stares off into space or loses track of things. I hope that makes sense? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm neglecting my responsibilities or anything like that. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you often talk excessively, even when others are trying to speak?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... well, I really try to be a good listener. I know how important it is to let others have their turn, especially the parents and my colleagues. I really do value what they have to say. \n\nBut... I suppose, if I'm being completely honest... sometimes I do get a little carried away? Like, I'll go into a parent conference or a staff meeting, and I'll tell myself, \"Just keep it brief, stick to the main points.\" But then... I don't know, I just get so passionate about my students, you know? And suddenly I hear myself giving three different examples, and then a story from my classroom, and then suggesting all these ideas that nobody even asked for... all while the other person is clearly trying to wrap things up. \n\nI always feel terrible afterward because I never mean to dominate the conversation or talk over anyone. I just want to make sure I'm being thorough and helpful! I really hope that doesn't make me sound rude or self-centered. I just have so much I want to share to support the kids. Does that sound like a normal thing to struggle with, just wanting to be too helpful sometimes?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your behavior is significantly interfering with your ability to function effectively in your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I really, really try to keep everything running smoothly. I want to be the best teacher and partner I can be. I always put my heart into it.\n\nBut... I guess... if I'm really looking at it... it has been causing some, well, some friction lately. I feel awful even saying this, but at work, some of the complaints from my colleagues got to the point where they actually put me on a... a corrective plan. I just wanted to be involved and help with everything, but I think I was overwhelming them. And at home... my spouse has mentioned that the caregiving routines we have are just getting so much harder. They said they feel like they have to manage my energy on top of everything else we have to do. \n\nI just feel terrible about it. I feel like I'm damaging the trust with my colleagues and my family, even though my intentions are always, always just to help and be supportive. I really don't want to be a burden to anyone. Is it normal to feel this way? I just hope you don't think less of me for struggling with this. I really am trying my best to fix it.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any physical complaints such as headaches or stomachaches?\n\nPatient: Oh, physical complaints? Um... no, not really. I mean, I feel pretty healthy overall! I make sure to take care of myself so I can be there for my students and my family. \n\nI don't really get headaches or stomachaches or anything like that. I mean, maybe a tiny headache if I haven't drank enough water during the school day, but that's just normal, right? Nothing out of the ordinary at all. I really try not to let little aches and pains bother me anyway. I wouldn't want to complain when there's so much work to be done. Is that the kind of thing you were looking for? I hope that's a good thing, that I'm feeling okay physically?\n\nDoctor: Do you often have difficulty sustaining focus during tasks or activities?\n\nPatient: Oh, focus? Well, I think I'm actually pretty good at staying engaged, especially when it comes to my students. I mean, you have to be, right? I really pride myself on being present for them. \n\nI don't think I have a hard time focusing... at least, not any more than anyone else who has a lot on their plate. Sometimes I might jump from one task to another, but that's just because I'm trying to get everything done and be as productive as possible. I want to make sure I'm not missing anything important! So, no, I wouldn't say I struggle with sustaining focus. I'm usually just very driven to tackle whatever needs doing. Does that make sense? I hope that's the right answer... I really do try to stay on top of things.\n\nDoctor: Do you often interrupt others or take over conversations without being aware of it?\n\nPatient: Oh, interrupting? Um... well, I really try to be a good listener. I know how important it is for people to feel heard, especially the parents of my students, and my colleagues, of course. I always want to be supportive.\n\nBut... I guess sometimes I might get a little too eager to help? Like, if I see my colleagues talking, or if I'm with a parent, or even at home when my spouse is talking with our relative... I just, I have this strong urge to jump in and offer a solution or, you know, just be useful. I think I'm helping, I really do! But... I suppose I do end up talking over them sometimes, or I kind of take charge of the conversation before they've even finished their thought. \n\nI never mean to be rude, I promise. It's just that I get so invested in whatever is going on and I want to fix things right away. It's just me trying to be a good team player, right? I really hope they know my heart is in the right place. Is it... is it really bad that I do that? I'm going to try so much harder to just wait my turn, I really am.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or have difficulty engaging in leisure activities quietly?\n\nPatient: Quiet leisure activities? Oh, um... well, I always try to look at it as just having a healthy amount of energy. It's a good thing to stay active at my age, right? I want to make sure I'm keeping busy and taking care of the house. \n\nBut... I guess, if I'm being completely honest... quiet time at home has become... well, it's almost impossible for me. I really, really don't want to be a nuisance, but if my spouse is just trying to watch a television show, or if our relative is resting... I just can't seem to just sit there in the silence. \n\nI'll start, you know, narrating whatever it is I'm doing in the other room, or I might start humming without even realizing it, or I'll just start moving things around to tidy up. I always think I'm being helpful! But then... it usually gets to the point where someone has to ask me to please just settle down. \n\nI feel awful when they have to say that. I never want to ruin their relaxation time, I just have this need to keep things moving and be productive. That makes sense, doesn't it? I hope you don't think I'm being inconsiderate to my family... I really am going to try to be better about giving them their quiet time.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your behavior is causing you significant distress or impairment in your daily life or relationships?\n\nPatient: Distress? Oh... well, I try not to look at it as distress. I always try to keep a positive attitude, you know? I want to be the person everyone can rely on. I love my job, and I love my family so much... \n\nBut... um... I guess, if I'm being completely honest... it has gotten a bit difficult lately. I really hate to admit this, I don't want you to think I'm doing a bad job, but... at work, well, some of the complaints from my colleagues actually got serious enough that my principal put me on a... a corrective plan. I'm working really, really hard on it, I promise! I just feel so awful about it because I'm only ever trying to help them out and be a good teacher. \n\nAnd then at home... my spouse sat me down and said that the caregiving routines for our relative are actually harder now. They said that on top of everything else they have to do, they feel like they have to manage *my* energy, too. I just... I hate the idea that I'm making things harder for them. \n\nI feel like I'm damaging the trust I have with my colleagues and my family, even though I'm just trying so hard to help everyone. It breaks my heart. I'm doing everything I can to fix it, I really am. Do you think... is this something I can get better at? I just want them to be happy with me again.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing these feelings of restlessness and excessive talking for as long as you can remember?\n\nPatient: Oh, for as long as I can remember? Um... well, I've always just considered myself a very enthusiastic person, you know? I was just a really eager learner when I was a kid. I loved school and I loved being involved. \n\nBut... I suppose, yes, it has been this way for a very long time. It's definitely not something that just started recently with the changes at home or anything like that. I actually remember... well, my mother used to joke about it. She would say that even back in elementary school, I was basically trying to teach the class from my seat. \n\nI was just so excited to participate! But... I guess I was always out of my chair, or talking, or... or jumping in before the teacher even finished giving directions. I really didn't mean to be disruptive, I was just so eager to get started and help out. It was just a part of who I was, even way back then. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound like I was a bad kid. I always wanted to be a good student, I just had so much energy and so many thoughts I wanted to share. Is it... is it normal to have been like that for so long? I really hope I haven't been a bother to people my whole life.\n\nDoctor: Do you often act on impulse without fully thinking through the consequences of your actions?\n\nPatient: Act on impulse? Oh... um, no, I really don't think I do that. I always try to be very careful and considerate of everyone around me, you know? I would just hate to do something that causes a problem for anyone else. \n\nI mean, I might jump into a task really quickly—like if I see a student struggling in my classroom or if something needs cleaning up at home, I'm right there doing it immediately. But I've always just viewed that as being proactive and eager to help, not... well, not impulsive. \n\nI really do try to think about how my actions affect my family and my colleagues. I want to be someone they can trust and rely on. So, no, I don't think I do things without thinking of the consequences. I'm always trying my absolute best to do the right thing... I hope that makes sense? I just really wouldn't want you to think I'm reckless or careless with my choices.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I hope I'm explaining that okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself interrupting others or blurting out answers before they've finished speaking?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh... um, well, I really hope this doesn't make me sound rude, because I truly always mean well. But... I guess, yes, that does happen to me sometimes. \n\nI just get so eager to assist, you know? Like, if I hear my colleagues or some of the parents talking at school, or even at home when my spouse is talking with a relative, I tend to just... jump right into the middle of their conversation. I always think I’m helping by jumping in with a thought or a solution, but then I end up sort of talking over them. Or I'll just take charge of the whole situation before they’ve even had a chance to finish what they were saying. \n\nI feel awful when I realize I've done it, because I really do value their input. I just get a little too enthusiastic about wanting to help out, I think. Is that... is that something you hear a lot from people? I really try my best to be a good listener.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I always try my absolute best to be professional and attentive, you know? But... I suppose I do have a bit of a habit of keeping moving, even when I'm seated. \n\nLike, if I'm sitting in a long IEP meeting for one of my students, or if I'm taking one of my admin classes... I guess my leg is usually bouncing under the table. Or I'll be clicking a pen, or just shifting around in my chair a whole lot. I just want to be helpful, you know? \n\nBut... I guess, if I'm being completely honest, things have been a little bit strained lately. I really try to be mindful and deliberate about that sort of thing so I don't create more of a mess for myself or my spouse. \n\nI hope that's an okay answer? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture, and I honestly can't think of a time recently where I've done something like what you're describing. Am I understanding what you mean correctly?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself easily distracted by your surroundings or unrelated thoughts?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... distracted? I really don't think so. I mean, as a special education teacher, I have to be so focused on my students and their needs. I always make sure to pull myself right back so I don't miss anything important. I really pride myself on being attentive to the people around me. \n\nI don't think I'm someone who just stares off into space or loses track of things. I hope that makes sense? Oh... um, that's a tough one. I really try my hardest to do both, you know? Pay attention to everything and make sure I'm behaving appropriately. I would hate to think I'm failing at either of those things, especially at my age and in my profession.

I try very, very hard to stay present for them, you know? \n\nI suppose sometimes my mind might wander a tiny bit if I'm doing something very routine, like grading the same type of worksheet over and over, but I think that's pretty normal, isn't it? I always make sure to pull myself right back so I don't miss anything important. I really pride myself on being attentive to the people around me. \n\nI don't think I'm someone who just stares off into space or loses track of things. I hope that makes sense? I just wouldn't want you to think I'm neglecting my responsibilities or anything like that. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you often talk excessively, even when others are trying to speak?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... well, I really try to be a good listener. I suppose, if I'm being completely honest... sometimes I do get a little carried away? Like, I'll go into a parent conference or a staff meeting, and I'll tell myself, \"Just keep it brief, stick to the main points.\" But then... I don't know, I just get so passionate about my students, you know? And suddenly I hear myself giving three different examples, and then a story from my classroom, and then suggesting all these ideas that nobody even asked for... all while the other person is clearly trying to wrap things up. \n\nI always feel terrible afterward because I never mean to dominate the conversation or talk over anyone. I just want to make sure I'm being thorough and helpful! I really hope that doesn't make me sound rude or self-centered. I just have so much I want to share to support the kids. I hope I'm explaining that okay.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself interrupting others or blurting out answers before they've finished speaking?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh... um, well, I really hope this doesn't make me sound rude, because I truly always mean well. But... I guess, yes, that does happen to me sometimes. \n\nI just get so eager to assist, you know? Like, if I hear my colleagues or some of the parents talking at school, or even at home when my spouse is talking with a relative, I tend to just... jump right into the middle of their conversation. I always think I’m helping by jumping in with a thought or a solution, but then I end up sort of talking over them. I really try my best to be a good listener.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're sitting still?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I always try my absolute best to be professional and attentive, you know? But... I suppose I do have a bit of a habit of keeping moving, even when I'm seated. \n\nLike, if I'm sitting in a long IEP meeting for one of my students, or if I'm taking one of my admin classes... I guess my leg is usually bouncing under the table. Or I'll be clicking a pen, or just shifting around in my chair a whole lot. Honestly, I do it so much that sometimes people will actually glance over at me, and that's usually when I finally realize I’m even doing it. \n\nI always feel terrible when they look over, because I really *am* paying close attention! I never want anyone to think I'm bored or being disrespectful. I just... I think those little movements sort of help me stay alert, maybe? Do you think that's just a normal way for people to cope with sitting through long meetings?\n\nDoctor: Are you finding it difficult to maintain your relationships with colleagues, spouse, or friends due to your behavior?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... um, well, I really hope this doesn't sound like I'm a difficult person to be around, because I truly care so much about everyone in my life. I just want to be helpful, you know? \n\nBut... I guess, if I'm being completely honest, things have been a little bit strained lately. I mean, at work, my colleagues have brought up some concerns, and... well, it actually got to the point where they had to put me on a corrective plan. I'm just trying my best to be supportive, but maybe I'm going about it the wrong way?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your behavior is affecting your overall quality of life?\n\nPatient: Oh, gosh...
